794-### Slice 2: mapped-writable stream fence (2 October 2026)
795-
796-**Status: implemented (feature build only), historically exercised on the debuggee, and still BLOCKED by later independent
797-review. Normal builds were previously verified unchanged (`NormalBuildIdentity=PASS`); taint stays until the user approves the
798-cutover. The later review found that fence retirement calls a DDI reserved for system use and that policy transition does not
799:cover a retained unmapped section handle that creates a view later. Residual limits are listed below and are NOT claimed fixed.**
800-
801:Goal: close the two confirmed leaks (a writable mapping that predates filter attachment, or that predates a policy scope
802-expansion) without private APIs or internal offsets, and prove that unapproved mapped bytes never reach the protected file.
803-
804-What exists (`StageFence.c`, hooks in `StageStream.c`, `Policy.c`, `Filter.c`, `Communication.c`, Inspector):
805-- **Scan and registry.** A scan of the bootstrap scope (`\SafeUpload\Escopo Monitorado` on every fixed NTFS volume) plus the
806:  prefixes of the current and candidate policy (union) registers streams whose section pointer reports a user-writable mapping
807-  (`MmDoesFileHaveUserWritableReferences` through an attribute-only open below the instance, or through the volume stack before
808:  attachment). This scan is not an atomic barrier: concurrent callbacks and retained section handles without views can escape
809-  its snapshot. Caps: 64 streams, 64 names.
810:- **Enforcement.** An unowned `IRP_PAGING_IO` write to a registered section pointer is refused (`STATUS_MEDIA_WRITE_PROTECTED`);
811-  a protected open of a registered name, and the service's WRITE open of it, is refused (`STATUS_SHARING_VIOLATION`); opens by
812-  file ID are refused only on a volume that holds an entry. All checks are in memory; no I/O in the create or write path.
813:- **Fixture E closed.** A writable data section created after the scan through a handle that predates attachment is refused at
814-  creation (`IRP_MJ_ACQUIRE_FOR_SECTION_SYNCHRONIZATION`, pre-operation name query, documented as allowed there). If no name
815:  can be resolved the feature build now refuses the section and increments `sectionNameUnresolved`.
816-- **Lifecycle (unresolved).** The current `FenceTryRelease` calls `FsRtlAcquireFileExclusive` before
817:  `CcPurgeCacheSection`, but Microsoft reserves `FsRtlAcquireFileExclusive` for system use and the cache purge does not
818-  purge mapped files. This is not a supported retirement proof. Independently, `MmDoesFileHaveUserWritableReferences`
819:  reports current mappings only: an existing writable section handle can still create a view later, and
820-  `SECTION_OBJECT_POINTERS.DataSectionObject` is opaque to filters and can change at any time. The unload guard refuses
821-  while any entry remains.
822-  A simple sticky-entry fallback was reviewed and rejected with the current fixed table: its 64-stream/name limits would
823-  become cumulative; after capacity exhaustion, the failed scan preserves the old table and quarantines covered names,
824:  while paging writes are still gated only by section-table membership. A newly encountered, unregistered section could
825-  therefore page out, and sticky references would also cause indefinite unload refusals. A safe replacement must address
826-  capacity and unknown-stream paging writes together.
827-- **Policy transition (unresolved).** `SetPolicy` holds the fence refresh mutex, keeps the old policy current and the
828-  candidate pending, then performs two checked pre-publication refreshes of the old-and-candidate union. A failed scan
829-  leaves the old policy current and preserves the last successfully installed fence table. This removes the former
830:  unchecked post-swap refresh, but the scans do not stop section callbacks or prevent a future view from an existing
831:  section handle. A minifilter post-op for `IRP_MJ_ACQUIRE_FOR_SECTION_SYNCHRONIZATION`
832:  runs before Memory Manager finishes creating the section, so it cannot serve as the scan barrier. The later release
833-  callback is the relevant endpoint, but its minifilter operation has no parameters and Microsoft warns that teardown can
834-  prevent a filter from observing the second operation. See Microsoft's
835-  [FS_FILTER_CALLBACKS contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/ns-ntifs-fs_filter_callbacks),
836:  [release parameters](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ifs/flt-parameters-for-irp-mj-release-for-section-synchronization),
837-  and [minifilter operation notes](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/fltkernel/ns-fltkernel-_flt_parameters).
838-  No safe tracker currently pairs overlapping CreateSection and SyncTypeOther operations or handles an unmatched release.
839:  A provisional registry keyed to active section acquisitions also cannot retire safely on a zero
840:  `MmDoesFileHaveUserWritableReferences` result plus cache purge: an application may retain the writable section object
841-  without a mapped view, then map a view after policy commit. The documented APIs provide no view-map callback or proof that
842:  such a section object is gone. Do not claim the transition closed. Broader create/write/rename
843-  admission races, removable/network coverage, failed-scan omissions and already-live views remain open. The load still
844-  scans before `FltStartFiltering` and fails closed.
845-- Inspector: `--admission-fence-status` (JSON, exit 4 after a failed scan) and `--admission-fence-refresh`.
846-
847-Review history (fresh Luna reviewers; reports kept as evidence):
848-1. [Review 1](evidence/2026-10-02/fence-review.txt) of the first implementation: BLOCKED, 7 blockers, 10 majors, 2 minors.
849:   Real defects fixed in the v2 rewrite (uninitialized name, scan zeroing, `FAST_MUTEX` held across file I/O, non-atomic table,
850-   union scope, pre-start fail-closed scan, file prefixes, deduplication, unload guard). One blocker was a false positive.
851-2. [Review 2](evidence/2026-10-02/fence-review2.txt) of v2: BLOCKED, 8 blockers, 4 majors, 1 minor. **Fixed:** a volume-root
852-   prefix scanned nothing; a trailing separator produced doubled-separator names; a full 260-character prefix was silently
853-   dropped; policy and fence generations could diverge (the transition now holds the refresh mutex end to end); opens by file ID
854-   were refused system-wide (now per volume); a read-only open whose name query fails for lack of resources; the service's
855-   write open of a quarantined name; the status JSON and exit code after a failed scan. **Not defects, or accepted limits**
856:   (see Residual limits): the zero-count fast path (it linearizes at install), reparse points (opens through them resolve to
857-   the target's name), mandatory unload, a hard link created into a candidate scope mid-scan, unbounded lower I/O in the scan.
858-   **A fix I tried and reverted:** refusing every read-only open whose name query fails turned each probe of a non-existent file
859-   into a sharing violation and broke PowerShell module loading under the fence; only resource-exhaustion failures are refused.
860:3. [Review 3](evidence/2026-10-02/fence-review3.txt) of the lifecycle purge and the writable-section refusal: BLOCKED, 1 blocker,
861-   1 major, 1 minor (brief: [fence-review3-brief.md](evidence/2026-10-02/worker-briefs/fence-review3-brief.md)). The purge mechanics, carry-over
862:   and reference counting, the transition mutex and its lock order, the volume-scoped refusals and the 128-byte status were confirmed.
863:   **Fixed (run 13/14):** (blocker) the writable-section refusal consulted only the CURRENT policy, so a mapping created on a file that was
864-   about to come into scope, after its pre-swap scan and before the swap, was allowed: the candidate policy is now published as pending
865-   (`SafeUploadPolicySetPending`, guarded by the policy lock, cleared before the snapshot is freed and at the swap) before the scan and the
866:   section refusal matches it too; (major) the volume-root scan produced names with doubled separators (separator only when needed now);
867-   (minor) `complete` is defined as "last scan succeeded and no removable/network scope skipped" and `reparseSkipped` is reported separately.
868-   **Not demonstrated by an experiment:** the transition race test (`Test-StagedPolicyTransitionRace.ps1`, a worker creating the first
869:   writable mapping at a random moment around the real agent's first policy push, over a fixture with 6,000 filler files so the scan is long)
870-   did **not** reproduce the unfixed behaviour on a control build of the pre-fix source (32 iterations, 0 writes succeeded,
871-   [calibration](evidence/2026-10-02/fence10-policyrace2-runA-verifier-gate.txt), [control](evidence/2026-10-02/fence10-policyrace3-runA-verifier-gate.txt)):
872:   the agent signals policy acceptance only about 1.4 to 1.8 s after launch and a leak needs a successful flush in the short interval
873-   between the swap and the post-swap registration, which the worker cannot observe without driver-side timestamps. The fix therefore rests
874-   on the review's code analysis plus the regression runs below, **not on a failing-then-passing test**. The test is kept as a regression
875-   check only. Review 3's conclusion that the purge path had no issue is superseded by the later DDI audit: VM runs showed the
876-   intended behavior on that specific build, but `FsRtlAcquireFileExclusive` is reserved for system use and cannot establish a
877-   documented supported lock contract for this minifilter. The historical runs therefore do not qualify the retirement path.
878-
879-**A leak the reviews did not find, found by experiment.** The first complete fence only refused writeback while an entry
880:existed, and a refresh pruned an entry once no user-writable mapping remained. [fence5-lifecycle-verifier](evidence/2026-10-02/fence5-lifecycle-verifier-gate.txt)
881-(run 9, Verifier on) reads the raw volume cluster of the fixture: after the old view was closed and one refresh, the Memory
882:Manager flushed the dirty pages and **the unapproved marker reached the disk with the driver still loaded**
883-(`Verdict_UnapprovedBytesReachedDisk=True`). The entry lifecycle was changed so that a stream is released only after
884-`CcPurgeCacheSection` discarded its dirty pages (see "Lifecycle" above). The same experiment on the final build:
885-[run 11](evidence/2026-10-02/fence7-lifecycle-verifier-gate.txt) and [run 12](evidence/2026-10-02/fence8-lifecycle-verifier-gate.txt)
886-keep the disk at the baseline bytes at every sample, 15 to 60 s after the refresh and after the unload and a fresh-handle
887:flush; the cached view also reads the baseline (the pages were discarded, not delayed); `streamsReleased=1`, the unload guard then
888-allows the unload, and refused paging writes stay at 9 (no retry storm, flat CPU). `CcPurgeCacheSection` is documented only for
889:file systems and says it will not purge mapped files; that it discards dirty pages of a section with no views is **observed on
890-Windows 10 19045.2965 NTFS, not documented**. The later audit also found the code's `FsRtlAcquireFileExclusive` call is
891-reserved for system use; this experiment is historical behavior on one build, not a supported retirement contract.

942-**Residual limits (documented, NOT claimed fixed):**
943-- Removable and network scopes are not scanned (`volumeScopesSkipped`, `complete:false`); item 4 owns them.
944:- A writable mapping of an **alternate data stream**, hard-link aliases readers outside the scope prefix, and volumes attached after
945-  load are covered only after the refresh that `InstanceSetup` queues (see Slice 3). Reparse points inside a scope are not followed.
946:- A writable section whose name cannot be resolved is now denied and counted. This does not close the independent mapping
947-  admission-to-scan window described below. At the run-16 source snapshot, mutating FSCTLs had a trusted Inspector/service
948-  bypass and allowed unresolved names; Round 5 removes that bypass and fails closed for unresolved or unsupported FSCTL scope.
949-  Those Round 5 changes are source-only and await review/build.
950-- **Release happens only at a refresh trigger** (load, policy push, the unload guard, `--admission-fence-refresh`); until then a
951-  quarantined name stays refused. There is no periodic retry yet.
--
953-  current release path cannot treat that as a supported guarantee: it calls `FsRtlAcquireFileExclusive`, which is reserved for
954-  system use. The service's READ open is not refused and may read dirty bytes while the stream remains fenced.
955-- Mandatory unload cannot be vetoed; a hard link created into a candidate scope while it is scanned; scans do synchronous lower I/O
956-  with no timeout and hold the refresh mutex (accepted on the disposable debuggee only, as with the slice 1 probe).
957-- The scan caps (64 streams, 64 names, 512 directories, 8192 files) fail closed.
958:- The review 3 fix (candidate policy visible to the section refusal) is not backed by a discriminating test (see review 3 above).
959-- Latency, USB, UNC/SMB, a real sync client, crash and recovery, and stress are **not measured here** (items 2 to 6).

1596-file object, so the unchanged all-object retirement condition still applies.

1598-cross-process duplicates); CLOSE releases those process references. The bounded
1599-implementation admits at most 16 locking processes per file object; excess lock

1684-### Implemented architecture
1685-
1686-`Filter.c` registers a full operation dispatcher for feature builds. `StageStream.c`
1687:owns the upper stream, namespace registry, cache, sections and retirement worker;
1688:`StageProtocol.c` reuses the existing allocation, seal, namespace transaction and
1689-publication-permit messages (wire protocol 18). `StageSecurity.c` retains original
1690-caller access checks. `StageDirectory.c` merges private names and snapshots upper
1691-metadata without opening the exclusively held backing. There is no reparse data
1692-path or borrowed NTFS FCB in the integrated feature.
1693-
1694-```mermaid
1695-flowchart LR
1696:    W[Writer at original destination path] --> U[Owned upper stream and cache]
1697-    U --> B[Private NTFS version on C:]
1698:    B --> S[Retire sections and close write access]
1699:    S --> I[Journal seal and inspect immutable snapshot]
1700-    I --> P[Authenticated approved publication]
1701-    P --> D[Destination filesystem]
1702-    O[Independent reader] --> D
1703-```
1704-
1705-Admission uses existing destination policy plus the recorded bootstrap test
1706-scope. A protected create succeeds only after original-token access checks and
1707:a flushed service `Allocated` manifest. The service chooses the GUID basename,
1708:sets private ACLs and seeds the file using aligned noncached, write-through I/O.
1709:The kernel opens that backing noncached with exclusive data sharing while mutable;
1710:any preexisting backing cache/section causes admission to fail. Only the owned
1711:upper cache is writable. Later read-only backing handles permit service inspection.
1712-A disconnected service cannot authorize new protected writes. The authenticated
1713-service needs an exact publication permit to write at the protected destination.
1714-
1715-The upper file object stays on the original destination volume, with SafeUpload's
1716-own `FSRTL_ADVANCED_FCB_HEADER`, resources and `SECTION_OBJECT_POINTERS`. All its
1717-operations are handled or rejected before legacy callbacks or the original
1718-filesystem can interpret its contexts. Fast I/O is refused. Paging I/O and Cc's
1719-`AdvanceOnly` EOF notification use separately allocated backing callback data;
1720:the original IRP, upper cache/sections and file object's volume stay intact.
1721:Only operation parameters and semantic paging/noncached flags are copied. A
1722-child read/write owns partial MDLs over the original request's locked pages;
1723-completion frees those partial MDLs, transfers status and releases rundown.
1724-Inline and asynchronous completion complete the upper request exactly once.
1725-The conservative original/backing stack-size admission check remains, but does
1726-not establish capacity for retargeting an existing IRP. See the boot Verifier
1727:failure and generated-I/O regression below. Neither foreign cache state nor
1728-`DeviceObject`/`Vpb` is modified.
1729-
1730-### Follow-up: native private logical identity
1731-
1732-Each live private view now has an immutable 128-bit logical file ID, derived
1733:from its first service allocation GUID with a nonzero high half. It is independent
1734-of subsequent immutable version GUIDs. A duplicate logical ID is rejected before
1735-admission. `FileIdInformation` and extended directory classes 60/63 return that
1736-same ID with the original NTFS volume's 64-bit serial. Initial physical-file and
1737-parent-directory IDs are captured below our instance, read-only, as identity
1738-snapshots; they are not an alias admission fence or journal authority. The native
1739:control reports volume serial `4AD66344D6632F7F`; do not substitute a zero-extended
1740-32-bit filesystem volume serial.
1741-
1742-`OpenFileById(ExtendedFileIdType)` resolves only an attached view belonging to
1743-the referenced requestor process and original instance. It snapshots the current
1744-name under the namespace resource, then rechecks that the exact same view still
1745-occupies that slot during CREATE. A rename/replacement race fails with sharing
1746-violation rather than allocating or opening another file. Views survive to unload,
1747-so the expected-view reference cannot dangle during admission. DELETE/share and
1748-access checks still use the existing owned CREATE path. Writable reopen of a
1749:sealed view allocates a new service version seeded from prior private content.
1750-Rename preserves the source logical ID; POSIX replacement detaches the target ID
1751-from fresh lookup while its held objects retain that ID and old bytes.
1752-
1753-Unknown writable physical IDs and unknown high-half/object IDs are denied.
1754-Physical read-only IDs retain legacy source inspection. Legacy 64-bit private
1755:directory/internal IDs remain zero; no physical file reference is invented.
1756-NTFS object-ID interoperability, physical alias canonicalization, boot-persistent
1757-logical IDs and authenticated reattachment remain unqualified. The live logical
1758-ID does not authorize another process or bypass current policy. Known private
1759-names/tombstones are considered before current prefix matching, so policy changes
1760-cannot silently redirect them to public bytes; follow-up service allocation still

1893-### Follow-up: pending namespace transaction mutation fence
1894-
1895-Ordinary writes previously checked ReadOnly/RenameExchange before locking the
1896-user buffer and taking the stream resource. Resize/allocation had a similar
1897-outer check. Rename sets its freeze under that resource, so an earlier check can
1898-be stale. Mutation now rechecks under the same resource; StageResize asserts
1899-exclusive ownership and enforces immutability/freeze for every caller. Allocation
1900:changes also recheck before touching the backing. New writable section creation
1901-is denied while a namespace transaction awaits acknowledgement. Already surviving
1902:writable mappings still write privately and still prevent sealing; this change
1903:does not treat handle closure or a pending rename as an immutable seal.

1940-### Follow-up: bounded seed allocation
1941-
1942-The kernel's 16 MiB version limit previously applied only after durable service
1943-allocation, which could copy an arbitrarily large physical or prior private
1944-source first. The allocator now checks the held source length before creating
1945-output and checks cumulative bytes before each write, including a source that
1946:grows while read. The Windows copy remains aligned/noncached/write-through;
1947-16 MiB is an exact multiple of its 64 KiB block. A failed seed creates no
1948-manifest and removes any partial output. Truncating dispositions still start
1949-empty without reading an oversized source. Prior private bytes/manifests are
1950:preserved. Protocol 18 and the seal condition are unchanged.

2624-| Identity | Implemented key and owner | Required extension |
2625-| --- | --- | --- |
2626-| Destination | Normalized original-volume path; durable monotonic `DestinationGeneration` per case-insensitive destination path | Stable volume identity plus file ID for existing objects; parent ID plus name slot for new/replacement objects; durable alias/tombstone records |
2627-| Private view | Referenced `PEPROCESS`, original instance, normalized name and immutable private 128-bit logical ID; PID is only existing protocol attribution | Durable view GUID bound to principal/session and driver boot epoch; explicit authenticated reattachment after recovery |
2628:| Version | Service transfer GUID/private basename; one upper stream/cache/backing per version | Preserve this identity independently of all aliases and namespace moves |
2629-| Open capability | `STAGE_HANDLE` on a specific `FILE_OBJECT`, pointing to its version | Keep duplicate/inherited handles bound to that version even when another version becomes current |
2630-
2631-Independent opens by the same writer share the current mutable version and
2632-`IoCheckShareAccess` state. Duplicated handles share a file object; its CLEANUP
2633-arrives only after its last handle closes, including in another process. The
--
2635-A duplicate grants access to that object, not the owner's entire directory view.
2636-Unrelated processes see physical, approved destination content. Published private
2637-views remain private views until unload; they do not silently switch held handles
2638-to the public object.
2639-
2640:After sealing, a writable reopen allocates a distinct service version. Nontruncating
2641-opens copy the prior **private** backing, including a previously blocked edit;
2642-CREATE/overwrite semantics select an empty new version where appropriate. A held
2643:read handle to an older sealed version keeps its original bytes. The registry
2644-retains old versions and process references until unload: at most 128 versions,
2645-16 MiB each. Exhaustion fails closed; reclamation is a remaining requirement.
2646-
2647-Supported native rename is a transaction over source and target slots on the
2648-same original volume. It checks DELETE and target-parent access, durably prepares
2649:both destination generations, changes the private mapping and source tombstone,
2650-then commits using existing messages. A linked value record in the same manifest
2651-retains source generation barriers (at most 16 moves per version). The service
2652-will not inspect/publish an older source or displaced target after restart.
2653-Lost replies reuse the transaction ID. Until acknowledgement, fresh opens,
2654:mutations and sealing remain held; existing mapped writes remain private.
2655-
2656-An ordinary replacement refuses an open private target. Native Ex
2657-REPLACE_IF_EXISTS|POSIX_SEMANTICS can displace a target whose open handles share
2658-DELETE; its old stream remains attached to those handles while its view leaves
2659:name lookup. Image sections and unacknowledged seals are rejected. The source's
2660-existing handles must all share DELETE. Directory overlays hide tombstones and
2661-displaced views without duplicating a reused active name. Creating into a source
2662-tombstone separates logical absence from physical existence. It now creates an
2663-empty private view even while the old public source exists; permission and durable
2664-tombstone checks are described below. Physical public-source removal remains pending.
--
2672-unqualified. Private directory notifications and concurrent directory mutation
2673-remain pending. Rename authorization captures the original request's thread/process subject,
2674-including impersonation. A forced SYSTEM-worker probe verifies this boundary;
2675-actual third-party filter stacks remain unqualified.
2676-
2677:### Lifetime, synchronization and exact seal
2678-
2679-Lock order is namespace resource, then upper stream resource. Ordinary reads,
2680-writes and size changes serialize on the stream resource. Cache/modified-writer
2681-callbacks use the separate paging resource; generated backing I/O owns rundown
2682-until lower completion (or the submitting callback observes inline completion).
2683-The registry spin lock only
2684-locates owned objects; entries cannot disappear until unregister drains callbacks.
2685-Name-provider callbacks use the namespace resource. The service is never called
2686:from paging completion. Current admission/namespace/seal messages can wait under
2687-the namespace resource; service self-I/O bypasses private-view lookup to avoid
2688-reentering that lock. Per-view locking and cancellation latency are future work.
2689-
2690:CLEANUP removes share participation and uninitializes that file object's cache
2691:map; it never seals. CLOSE releases its handle context and object count. A 250 ms
2692-worker attempts conservative retirement while holding namespace then stream locks.
2693-**Every condition below must succeed, in this order:**
2694-
2695-1. No open share participants; `MmCanFileBeTruncated(Sections, NULL)` says no user
2696:   mappings, section references, images or relevant write probes remain.
2697:2. Flush the owned cache and backing successfully. Purge the owned cache, then
2698:   require all shared-cache/data/image-section pointers to be NULL and the upper
2699:   file-object count to be zero. A remaining read-only section also delays sealing.
2700:3. Drain all paging/AdvanceOnly rundown references; flush the backing again.
2701-4. Close the last kernel backing write handle, irrevocably mark the version
2702-   read-only, and reopen it read-only. A reopen failure keeps all new opens blocked
2703:   and retries; it cannot leave a partially admitted backing or authorize a seal.
2704:5. Obtain the durable service seal acknowledgement. Retry the same immutable
2705-   version after disconnect/reply loss; idempotent acknowledgement never resets an
2706-   existing inspection, block, approval or release. Only then mark `Sealed` locally.
2707-
2708-The all-object condition deliberately favors correctness over save latency. It
2709:must not be replaced by a writer-handle counter or section acquire/release counter.
2710:Microsoft documents [cache uninitialization](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ccuninitializecachemap),
2711:[purge restrictions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ccpurgecachesection)
2712-and [the NULL truncation check](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-mmcanfilebetruncated).
2713-
2714-Initial CREATE cancellation is checked before allocation and before publishing an
2715:upper object. A canceled/lost allocation may leave a durable unsealed orphan, never
2716-an approved save. Ordinary synchronous operations complete their current action;
2717-a prepared namespace transaction remains owned by the stream until commit/abort
2718-acknowledgement. Full cancel-safe queued I/O and fault injection remain unqualified.
2719-
2720-### Publication and recovery ordering
2721-
2722-The existing service flow is `Allocated -> Sealed -> Inspecting -> Approved ->
2723:Publishing -> Released`, with `Blocked`/`Retained` outcomes and recovery `Unsealed`.
2724-`SealAsync` requires matching owner attribution and backing path and rejects a
2725:pending rename. The publisher holds the sealed file without writer sharing, scans
2726:its inspection snapshot, and rechecks the sealed digest and policy before copying
2727-that inspected snapshot. `AllowedWithoutInspection` never grants publication.
2728-The kernel's authenticated, expiring permit binds transfer ID, digest and exact
2729-temporary/destination paths, admits one temporary create and consumes one native
2730-rename. Approved Windows output uses one exclusive temporary opened with WRITE
2731:and DELETE, flushed then replaced via FileRenameInfoEx (REPLACE|POSIX) on the same
2732:held handle. Existing public readers/mappings retain old bytes. Read-only targets
2733-are refused; unsupported stacks have no copy fallback. The kernel trusts the authenticated service's digest decision; it does not
2734-re-hash the output in a paging callback. Bytes in the public temporary are already
2735:approved; it is flushed before final rename and durable `Released` bookkeeping.
2736-
2737-The journal now assigns increasing destination generations without changing wire
2738-messages. Only the latest durably allocated generation may enter Inspecting, Approved or
2739-Publishing. Even a subsequently canceled CREATE supersedes older approvals; restoring
2740-an earlier version requires an explicit new save, not deleting its newer manifest. An older justification cannot replace a later edit. `Publishing`
2741-reserves the destination against new allocation and prepared renames until the
2742-outcome is durable. A pending rename reserves both names. All checks and manifest
2743-replacement share the journal gate; generations survive service restart. Released
2744-manifests must not be garbage-collected without a durable generation/tombstone
2745:replacement. Legacy duplicate generation-zero histories fail closed.
2746-
2747-Ordinary source inspection allows delete sharing while excluding ordinary writes.
2748-Its read handle pins the file being read across an approved directory replacement;
2749-hash and scan use the same resulting bytes. Together with POSIX destination replacement, this prevents the existing source
2750-classifier from vetoing publication while it pins a prior public version.
2751:The sealed version's own service handle still excludes writes and deletion.
2752-
2753-| Failure | Implemented behavior | Remaining acceptance |
2754-| --- | --- | --- |
2755:| Service disappears | New allocations denied; existing owned/mapped writes stay private; restart changes Allocated to Unsealed; later kernel retirement can durably seal it | Fault injection at every request/reply boundary and bounded offline resource use |
2756:| Seal reply lost | Backing stays read-only; idempotent durable seal retries | Systematic disconnect test at the exact lost-reply boundary |
2757:| Voluntary driver unload | Refuses live upper objects/sections; stops worker and drains paging, drops instance references before unregister | Stress with concurrent unload/create and filter-stack changes |
2758-| SCM service stop | Feature registration rejects mandatory service-stop unload | Boot/shutdown lifecycle qualification |
2759:| Driver loss/reboot | Private ACLs and manifests survive; an unsealed version cannot become approved from a missing driver; interrupted inspection/approval retained; Publishing reconciles destination digest | Durable private namespace/view reattachment, user recovery/export and crash/power-loss matrix |
2760-| Rename interrupted | Pending record retains bytes and blocks publication; live driver retries same commit/abort | Reconciliation after driver loss needs durable namespace/tombstone authority |
2761-
2762-The feature uses the documented
2763-[service-stop registration flag](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/fltkernel/ns-fltkernel-_flt_registration).
2764:A crash may lose dirty cache bytes; durable recovery promises no automatic approval,
2765:not recovery of every unflushed application byte. File flush plus atomic same-volume
2766-manifest replacement is the existing protocol durability model; sudden power loss
2767-and directory-entry persistence have not been qualified. No automatic stage or
2768-journal garbage collection is introduced.

2974-
2975-Architectural rationale: a reparse changes the file object's volume. The new
2976-experiment completes a CREATE on the original stack using its own upper stream
2977:and section objects, and uses a separate kernel backing handle for local data.
2978:It does not modify `DeviceObject`, `Vpb`, another filesystem's FCB, or its cache.
2979-All operations on an owned upper file object are handled or rejected before
2980-they can reach the original filesystem. Paging requests preserve their flags,
2981-MDLs and asynchronous completion when redirected to the local backing instance.
2982-The backing stack-size requirement is checked before accepting the create.
2983-See Microsoft's [I/O parameter-block contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/fltkernel/ns-fltkernel-_flt_io_parameter_block),
2984:[cache-map ownership contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ccinitializecachemap),
2985-and OSR's [same-stack isolation architecture](https://www.osr.com/nt-insider/2017-issue2/introduction-standard-isolation-minifilters/).
2986-
2987-Baseline checkpoint: existing reparse path redirects an `S:` create to a private
2988-`C:` backing file. Native volume identity is `C:` and native rename returns
2989-Win32 error 17. Rewriting `FileVolumeNameInformation` in the minifilter did not
2990-affect that query.
2991-This failure belongs to the old reparse architecture; the separate isolation
2992-experiment is the replacement under qualification.
2993-Baseline: commit `9dbdb16`, 216 Windows agent tests, both WDK builds and limited
2994:volatile Verifier tests passed. See the later dated sections for exact coverage.
2995-
2996-## Architecture decision: preserve the original stack, own the private stream
2997-
2998-The writing application's handle must stay on the destination's original stack.
2999-Its stream data must have a separate local backing object. The driver owns the
3000:upper FCB, share state, cache and section pointers from CREATE through final
3001-CLOSE. It completes or rejects every operation on an upper object before a
3002-foreign filesystem can interpret that FCB. It never borrows the backing NTFS
3003-FCB or changes `DeviceObject`/`Vpb` to manufacture volume identity.
3004-
3005-```mermaid
3006-flowchart LR
3007:    A[Writing app] --> U[Original-stack upper object and cache]
3008-    U --> B[Private local NTFS backing]
3009-    O[Other apps and sync clients] --> F[Original destination filesystem]
3010-    F --> D[Approved destination bytes]
3011-```
3012-
--
3022-
3023-- Native class-58 volume queries and `GetFinalPathNameByHandle` retain the
3024-  original destination identity. Native same-volume rename needs no copy/move
3025-  fallback. A name provider and explicit invalidation keep existing handles'
3026-  current names consistent after virtual rename.
3027:- Ordinary cached I/O and writable mappings share one upper cache. Paging
3028-  requests go to the local backing with their MDL, paging and synchronous flags
3029-  preserved. The documented backing-device stack-size requirement is checked
3030-  before CREATE; unsupported stacks are rejected.
3031:- The backing handle uses noncached I/O. Growth explicitly zeroes the extended
3032:  range and preserves an old partial sector before increasing upper VDL. Cc's
3033:  `AdvanceOnly` EOF notification updates backing VDL; it must not resize the
3034:  upper stream or recreate a cache on a cleaned handle. See Microsoft's
3035-  [EOF notification contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ifs/flt-parameters-for-irp-mj-set-information).
3036:- CLEANUP removes a handle's share/cache participation; CLOSE retires its file
3037-  object. A mapped view can still write after ordinary handles close. Unload
3038:  refuses live file objects, mappings or cache/section state, then drains
3039-  callbacks before freeing the stream. Instance references are released before
3040-  unregister to avoid a reference-cycle wait.
3041-- Root and backing-file SYSTEM-only ACLs protect retained bytes after unload.
3042-  These ACLs supplement kernel admission; they do not supply a publication
3043-  authorization or a complete protected-destination policy.
--
3070-   underlying filesystem handles the create. Direct writes, paging writes,
3071-   truncation, rename, hard link, and file-ID opens cannot bypass this gate.
3072-2. The private staging file is on a local fixed volume. Its path is never inside
3073-   a protected sync folder. The service records the destination and originating
3074-   user in a durable journal before returning the staging path to the driver.
3075:3. The driver tracks all writable handles on a staged stream. CLEANUP never seals a version: all upper file objects and sections must
3076:   retire, cached/paging I/O must drain, and backing write access must close. The service acquires a read handle that excludes
3077-   writers for the entire scan and copy. A later open starts a new version.
3078-4. The service releases only `Approved` after actual content inspection under
3079-   the current policy. `AllowedWithoutInspection` is **not** approval for a
3080-   staged transfer. A transfer also stays local if the destination is no longer
3081-   in policy scope when publication is attempted.
3082-5. The service writes an approved version to a temporary file at the target,
3083:   flushes it, and renames it into place. Only the service's authenticated
3084-   publication path may bypass the destination gate. The published bytes must
3085-   be the same locked version that was inspected.
3086:6. Stage creation, sealing, approval, publication, and retention have durable
3087-   states. On restart, the service reconciles each state. It must not treat an
3088-   interrupted inspection as an approval, and it must not lose a user file.
3089-7. A user can justify a blocked transfer only against its exact transfer ID
3090-   and inspected version. A justification may release that version; it must
3091-   never grant a general process or destination bypass.

3196-## Recovery and journal hardening (30 September 2026)
3197-
3198:Recovery now puts an interrupted `Allocated` transfer in a distinct `Unsealed`
3199-state. It cannot enter inspection or publication. The journal records whether
3200:the transfer was ever sealed, so an older `Retained` manifest without seal
3201-provenance also cannot be published. This closes a recovery path where an
3202-unfinished writer's bytes could otherwise become eligible for inspection.
3203-
3204-The prototype journal moved to `%ProgramData%\SafeUpload\staging-journal`.
3205-On Windows, its directory and manifests get explicit service, SYSTEM, and
3206-Administrators ACLs. Prototype startup rejects a parent that grants ordinary
3207-users child-deletion rights; a LocalSystem service also rejects existing
3208-journal objects owned by other identities. The debugger VM passed 22 focused
3209-tests and the complete 192-test agent suite. The cross-volume debuggee test
3210:passed after the move, including `Unsealed` recovery and restoration of the
3211-original driver hash.

3223-## Final writer and later version probe (30 September 2026)
3224-
3225-Protocol 14 uses a kernel-issued `STAGE_SEAL` message after the last writable
3226-handle cleans up. The driver counts staged writers and blocks another writer
3227:while a seal request is in flight. A writable memory-mapped view delays sealing
3228:after its file handle closes; a prototype worker checks the section's writable
3229:references and seals after the view disappears. The agent journals `Sealed`,
3230-notifies `Analyzing`, inspects that version, and publishes a clean version with
3231-its connected-port process identity. A blocked or uninspectable version remains
3232:local. A service restart changes interrupted `Allocated` to `Unsealed` and
3233:requires the real driver seal signal before inspection.
3234-
3235:After a sealed version, the same writer's next writable open allocates a new
3236-transfer ID. `FILE_OPEN` and `FILE_OPEN_IF` copy the prior stage into the new
3237-local stage; truncating dispositions start empty. A sensitive append did not
3238-change the already published `alphabeta` destination bytes, and a later clean
3239-overwrite published only `clean replacement`. The debuggee test also passed
3240-two concurrent handles, a mapped write without an extra file reopen, and a

--
3374-Protocol 18 replaces the single-phase retarget with durable prepare and
3375-commit/abort. Preparation records both the original transfer and pending
3376-destination without authorizing either for publication. Journal transitions
3377-and the publisher reject a pending rename. The driver changes its virtual
3378-namespace, then commits that exact request ID and destination; it holds the
3379:mapping against later writes and sealing until commit acknowledgement. Its
3380-worker retries a lost commit or abort after reconnection. Completion is
3381-idempotent, rejects a different owner, transaction or destination, and never
3382-invents a prepare. Recovery preserves an unresolved rename instead of assuming
3383-it completed. After driver loss, that unresolved version stays local pending
3384-an explicit recovery path; automatic namespace reconstruction is unfinished.
3385-
3386-The full Windows agent suite passed 216 tests, including pending-rename
3387-restart, replay, abort, wrong-owner/destination, and publication-before-commit
3388:cases. Both WDK build modes passed; the application build had zero warnings
3389-and errors. The native local rename/version tests passed. The publisher loop
3390:also keeps running if a rename races its attempt to mark a failed seal retained.

3548-
3549-- `StageAdmit` checks the installed normalized fence-name table before the
3550-  current-policy out-of-scope return and before the service/publication path.
3551-  The volume quarantine remains scoped after policy classification, so an
3552-  unrelated name on that volume stays available. Publication rename retains
3553:  its target-volume quarantine refusal. The writable-section callback checks
3554:  installed section-object identity and carried-over exact names before
3555-  current or pending policy matching; unresolved names are denied. The former
3556:  Inspector service exemption on writable-section admission is removed.
3557:  Unowned IRP and MDL reads now deny an installed fenced section-object
3558-  identity; fenced fast-I/O reads are sent through the IRP read gate. These
3559-  checks use only the nonpaged SOP table and do not query names on paging or
3560-  top-level paths. Owned `StageStream` reads remain on their existing path.
3561-  Mutating FSCTLs no longer have an Inspector PID bypass. Safe PASSIVE-level
3562-  NTFS requests retain normalized-name and hard-link-alias checks; unsafe,
3563-  unresolved, or unsupported-filesystem cases are denied and counted because
3564:  an out-of-scope cache name cannot rule out a protected alias. This remains a
3565-  bounded list of FSCTLs, not a general device-control gate. Extant user views
3566-  can still read resident pages without a filter callback, so the read checks
3567-  do not close the mapped-view privacy blocker.
3568-- Policy transition rejects an unmatched or unavailable candidate prefix,
3569-  requested removable/network scan scope, and reparses in a required candidate

  2700	3. Drain all paging/AdvanceOnly rundown references; flush the backing again.
  2701	4. Close the last kernel backing write handle, irrevocably mark the version
  2702	   read-only, and reopen it read-only. A reopen failure keeps all new opens blocked
  2703	   and retries; it cannot leave a partially admitted backing or authorize a seal.
  2704	5. Obtain the durable service seal acknowledgement. Retry the same immutable
  2705	   version after disconnect/reply loss; idempotent acknowledgement never resets an
  2706	   existing inspection, block, approval or release. Only then mark `Sealed` locally.
  2707	
  2708	The all-object condition deliberately favors correctness over save latency. It
  2709	must not be replaced by a writer-handle counter or section acquire/release counter.
  2710	Microsoft documents [cache uninitialization](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ccuninitializecachemap),
  2711	[purge restrictions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ccpurgecachesection)
  2712	and [the NULL truncation check](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-mmcanfilebetruncated).
  2713	
  2714	Initial CREATE cancellation is checked before allocation and before publishing an
  2715	upper object. A canceled/lost allocation may leave a durable unsealed orphan, never
  2716	an approved save. Ordinary synchronous operations complete their current action;
  2717	a prepared namespace transaction remains owned by the stream until commit/abort
  2718	acknowledgement. Full cancel-safe queued I/O and fault injection remain unqualified.
  2719	
  2720	### Publication and recovery ordering
  2721	
  2722	The existing service flow is `Allocated -> Sealed -> Inspecting -> Approved ->
  2723	Publishing -> Released`, with `Blocked`/`Retained` outcomes and recovery `Unsealed`.
  2724	`SealAsync` requires matching owner attribution and backing path and rejects a
  2725	pending rename. The publisher holds the sealed file without writer sharing, scans
  2726	its inspection snapshot, and rechecks the sealed digest and policy before copying
  2727	that inspected snapshot. `AllowedWithoutInspection` never grants publication.
  2728	The kernel's authenticated, expiring permit binds transfer ID, digest and exact
  2729	temporary/destination paths, admits one temporary create and consumes one native
  2730	rename. Approved Windows output uses one exclusive temporary opened with WRITE
  2731	and DELETE, flushed then replaced via FileRenameInfoEx (REPLACE|POSIX) on the same
  2732	held handle. Existing public readers/mappings retain old bytes. Read-only targets
  2733	are refused; unsupported stacks have no copy fallback. The kernel trusts the authenticated service's digest decision; it does not
  2734	re-hash the output in a paging callback. Bytes in the public temporary are already
  2735	approved; it is flushed before final rename and durable `Released` bookkeeping.
  2736	
  2737	The journal now assigns increasing destination generations without changing wire
  2738	messages. Only the latest durably allocated generation may enter Inspecting, Approved or
  2739	Publishing. Even a subsequently canceled CREATE supersedes older approvals; restoring
  2740	an earlier version requires an explicit new save, not deleting its newer manifest. An older justification cannot replace a later edit. `Publishing`
  2741	reserves the destination against new allocation and prepared renames until the
  2742	outcome is durable. A pending rename reserves both names. All checks and manifest
  2743	replacement share the journal gate; generations survive service restart. Released
  2744	manifests must not be garbage-collected without a durable generation/tombstone
  2745	replacement. Legacy duplicate generation-zero histories fail closed.
  2746	
  2747	Ordinary source inspection allows delete sharing while excluding ordinary writes.
  2748	Its read handle pins the file being read across an approved directory replacement;
  2749	hash and scan use the same resulting bytes. Together with POSIX destination replacement, this prevents the existing source
  2750	classifier from vetoing publication while it pins a prior public version.
  2751	The sealed version's own service handle still excludes writes and deletion.
  2752	
  2753	| Failure | Implemented behavior | Remaining acceptance |
  2754	| --- | --- | --- |
  2755	| Service disappears | New allocations denied; existing owned/mapped writes stay private; restart changes Allocated to Unsealed; later kernel retirement can durably seal it | Fault injection at every request/reply boundary and bounded offline resource use |
  2756	| Seal reply lost | Backing stays read-only; idempotent durable seal retries | Systematic disconnect test at the exact lost-reply boundary |
  2757	| Voluntary driver unload | Refuses live upper objects/sections; stops worker and drains paging, drops instance references before unregister | Stress with concurrent unload/create and filter-stack changes |
  2758	| SCM service stop | Feature registration rejects mandatory service-stop unload | Boot/shutdown lifecycle qualification |
  2759	| Driver loss/reboot | Private ACLs and manifests survive; an unsealed version cannot become approved from a missing driver; interrupted inspection/approval retained; Publishing reconciles destination digest | Durable private namespace/view reattachment, user recovery/export and crash/power-loss matrix |
  2760	| Rename interrupted | Pending record retains bytes and blocks publication; live driver retries same commit/abort | Reconciliation after driver loss needs durable namespace/tombstone authority |
  2761	
  2762	The feature uses the documented
  2763	[service-stop registration flag](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/fltkernel/ns-fltkernel-_flt_registration).
  2764	A crash may lose dirty cache bytes; durable recovery promises no automatic approval,
  2765	not recovery of every unflushed application byte. File flush plus atomic same-volume
  2766	manifest replacement is the existing protocol durability model; sudden power loss
  2767	and directory-entry persistence have not been qualified. No automatic stage or
  2768	journal garbage collection is introduced.
  2769	
  2770	### Destination contract and remaining acceptance
  2771	
  2772	The admitted data path requires local NTFS at destination and backing, a power-of-two
  2773	backing sector size 512..65536, and the checked backing stack-size relation. Network
  2774	volumes are rejected. The VM proves C: backing with C: and disposable S: NTFS
  2775	destinations. ReFS/FAT/exFAT, physical USB surprise removal, SMB/UNC/redirectors,
  2776	cloud sync clients, and third-party filter stacks are not qualified. A redirector
  2777	needs its own demonstrated section, paging, identity and reconnect contracts; it
  2778	must not be enabled by removing the NTFS/stack guards.
  2779	
  2780	Before deployment, complete destination/view/alias identities and physical-source
  2781	namespace transactions, delete/link/metadata/locking/notification semantics, driver-loss
  2782	recovery with user access to retained files, resource reclamation, disk-full and
  2783	corrupt-journal handling, permit replay/spoof/expiry kernel fault tests,
  2784	Explorer/Office saves, real destination stacks, and
  2785	boot-time DDI/filter Verifier plus stress. Keep staging opt-in and retain taint
  2786	until its remaining responsibilities are demonstrably replaced.
  2787

  1893	### Follow-up: pending namespace transaction mutation fence
  1894	
  1895	Ordinary writes previously checked ReadOnly/RenameExchange before locking the
  1896	user buffer and taking the stream resource. Resize/allocation had a similar
  1897	outer check. Rename sets its freeze under that resource, so an earlier check can
  1898	be stale. Mutation now rechecks under the same resource; StageResize asserts
  1899	exclusive ownership and enforces immutability/freeze for every caller. Allocation
  1900	changes also recheck before touching the backing. New writable section creation
  1901	is denied while a namespace transaction awaits acknowledgement. Already surviving
  1902	writable mappings still write privately and still prevent sealing; this change
  1903	does not treat handle closure or a pending rename as an immutable seal.

  2621	
  2622	### Identity and namespace
  2623	
  2624	| Identity | Implemented key and owner | Required extension |
  2625	| --- | --- | --- |
  2626	| Destination | Normalized original-volume path; durable monotonic `DestinationGeneration` per case-insensitive destination path | Stable volume identity plus file ID for existing objects; parent ID plus name slot for new/replacement objects; durable alias/tombstone records |
  2627	| Private view | Referenced `PEPROCESS`, original instance, normalized name and immutable private 128-bit logical ID; PID is only existing protocol attribution | Durable view GUID bound to principal/session and driver boot epoch; explicit authenticated reattachment after recovery |
  2628	| Version | Service transfer GUID/private basename; one upper stream/cache/backing per version | Preserve this identity independently of all aliases and namespace moves |
  2629	| Open capability | `STAGE_HANDLE` on a specific `FILE_OBJECT`, pointing to its version | Keep duplicate/inherited handles bound to that version even when another version becomes current |
  2630	
  2631	Independent opens by the same writer share the current mutable version and
  2632	`IoCheckShareAccess` state. Duplicated handles share a file object; its CLEANUP
  2633	arrives only after its last handle closes, including in another process. The
  2634	referenced process identity prevents PID reuse from inheriting a private view.
  2635	A duplicate grants access to that object, not the owner's entire directory view.
  2636	Unrelated processes see physical, approved destination content. Published private
  2637	views remain private views until unload; they do not silently switch held handles
  2638	to the public object.
  2639	
  2640	After sealing, a writable reopen allocates a distinct service version. Nontruncating
  2641	opens copy the prior **private** backing, including a previously blocked edit;
  2642	CREATE/overwrite semantics select an empty new version where appropriate. A held
  2643	read handle to an older sealed version keeps its original bytes. The registry
  2644	retains old versions and process references until unload: at most 128 versions,
  2645	16 MiB each. Exhaustion fails closed; reclamation is a remaining requirement.
  2646	
  2647	Supported native rename is a transaction over source and target slots on the
  2648	same original volume. It checks DELETE and target-parent access, durably prepares
  2649	both destination generations, changes the private mapping and source tombstone,
  2650	then commits using existing messages. A linked value record in the same manifest
  2651	retains source generation barriers (at most 16 moves per version). The service
  2652	will not inspect/publish an older source or displaced target after restart.
  2653	Lost replies reuse the transaction ID. Until acknowledgement, fresh opens,
  2654	mutations and sealing remain held; existing mapped writes remain private.
  2655	
  2656	An ordinary replacement refuses an open private target. Native Ex
  2657	REPLACE_IF_EXISTS|POSIX_SEMANTICS can displace a target whose open handles share
  2658	DELETE; its old stream remains attached to those handles while its view leaves
  2659	name lookup. Image sections and unacknowledged seals are rejected. The source's
  2660	existing handles must all share DELETE. Directory overlays hide tombstones and
  2661	displaced views without duplicating a reused active name. Creating into a source
  2662	tombstone separates logical absence from physical existence. It now creates an
  2663	empty private view even while the old public source exists; permission and durable
  2664	tombstone checks are described below. Physical public-source removal remains pending.
  2665	
  2666	Hard links require alias entries for one destination identity, not independent
  2667	path-keyed publication rights. Short names, physical file-ID opens and reparse aliases
  2668	must resolve before policy/view lookup. Links, delete/disposition, alternate
  2669	streams, oplocks and unsupported metadata operations fail explicitly;
  2670	unknown writable file-ID admission is denied; known private 128-bit IDs use owned admission. This is not complete enforcement for
  2671	preexisting external links or case-sensitive directories: those namespaces are
  2672	unqualified. Private directory notifications and concurrent directory mutation
  2673	remain pending. Rename authorization captures the original request's thread/process subject,
  2674	including impersonation. A forced SYSTEM-worker probe verifies this boundary;
  2675	actual third-party filter stacks remain unqualified.
  2676	
  2677	### Lifetime, synchronization and exact seal
  2678	
  2679	Lock order is namespace resource, then upper stream resource. Ordinary reads,
  2680	writes and size changes serialize on the stream resource. Cache/modified-writer
  2681	callbacks use the separate paging resource; generated backing I/O owns rundown
  2682	until lower completion (or the submitting callback observes inline completion).
  2683	The registry spin lock only
  2684	locates owned objects; entries cannot disappear until unregister drains callbacks.
  2685	Name-provider callbacks use the namespace resource. The service is never called
  2686	from paging completion. Current admission/namespace/seal messages can wait under
  2687	the namespace resource; service self-I/O bypasses private-view lookup to avoid
  2688	reentering that lock. Per-view locking and cancellation latency are future work.
  2689	
  2690	CLEANUP removes share participation and uninitializes that file object's cache
  2691	map; it never seals. CLOSE releases its handle context and object count. A 250 ms
  2692	worker attempts conservative retirement while holding namespace then stream locks.
  2693	**Every condition below must succeed, in this order:**
  2694	
  2695	1. No open share participants; `MmCanFileBeTruncated(Sections, NULL)` says no user
  2696	   mappings, section references, images or relevant write probes remain.
  2697	2. Flush the owned cache and backing successfully. Purge the owned cache, then
  2698	   require all shared-cache/data/image-section pointers to be NULL and the upper
  2699	   file-object count to be zero. A remaining read-only section also delays sealing.
  2700	3. Drain all paging/AdvanceOnly rundown references; flush the backing again.
  2701	4. Close the last kernel backing write handle, irrevocably mark the version
  2702	   read-only, and reopen it read-only. A reopen failure keeps all new opens blocked
  2703	   and retries; it cannot leave a partially admitted backing or authorize a seal.
  2704	5. Obtain the durable service seal acknowledgement. Retry the same immutable
  2705	   version after disconnect/reply loss; idempotent acknowledgement never resets an
  2706	   existing inspection, block, approval or release. Only then mark `Sealed` locally.
  2707	
  2708	The all-object condition deliberately favors correctness over save latency. It
  2709	must not be replaced by a writer-handle counter or section acquire/release counter.
  2710	Microsoft documents [cache uninitialization](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ccuninitializecachemap),

codex
## ACCEPT WITH CONDITIONS — source review only

HEAD `bd4d02fb` differs from parent `7c316ebb` only in `StageStream.c`: it splits cache flushing from the full backing flush and uses the cache-only part during growth.

**Ordering and confidentiality:** The new `StageFlushCache` still calls `CcFlushCache` and checks its status. `StageZeroGrowth` calls it before reading a partial sector and writing the zeroed extension; `StageResize` updates the backing EOF and upper file size/VDL only after those writes complete. The aligned noncached writes, partial-tail preservation, and VDL sequence are unchanged ([flush split](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:1070>), [growth path](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:1504>), [resize commit](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:1548>)). The backing is opened noncached and rejected if it already has a cache or section map ([backing open](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:1142>)).

The definite durability change is narrower: growth no longer synchronizes the lower storage cache for earlier dirty data as a side effect. `CcFlushCache` can wait for cached data to be flushed; `FltFlushBuffers` synchronously issues a flush and synchronizes the underlying storage cache ([Microsoft: CcFlushCache](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/nf-ntifs-ccflushcache), [Microsoft: FltFlushBuffers](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/fltkernel/nf-fltkernel-fltflushbuffers)). That stable-media guarantee remains on explicit flush, write-through, cleanup, and seal/drain paths ([write-through and flush dispatch](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:1628>), [cleanup](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:2094>), [drain](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:2233>)). I find no new live-read exposure from this change. It does not establish a power-loss guarantee for the zeroed extension: the old device flush occurred before the zero writes, and the project’s durability notes already leave sudden-power-loss persistence unqualified ([design note](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/STAGED-WRITES.md:2765>)).

**C02 and deadlock scope:** The dump shows `FltFlushBuffers` in the `StageFlush → StageZeroGrowth → StageResize` call path from `FsRtlSetFileSize` during section creation ([stack](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/evidence/2026-10-07/sol-c02diag1-cdb-initial2-decoded.txt:336>)). The inner flush reached the `viostor`/`DiskFlushComplete` path; the callback evidence is not a FileInfo PRE block ([inner IRP](</tmp/sol-c02diag1-cdb-fileinfo-decoded.txt:110>)). `TopLevelIrp` was 1 and the backing file object had no Shared Cache Map ([dump details](</tmp/sol-c02diag1-cdb-flush-decoded.txt:127>)). `KiBugCheckData` is all zero ([dump](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/evidence/2026-10-07/sol-c02diag1-cdb-initial2-decoded.txt:60>)); this proves a wait in the flush path, not why the lower completion stalled. The candidate removes that specific synchronous device flush from growth. `CcFlushCache` remains waitable, so this is not proof that all growth I/O is deadlock-free.

The same full flush remains in cleanup and seal/drain. If the lower stall also affects those operations, source close or sealing could still wait; that is existing behavior and was not changed. The asynchronous paging/AdvanceOnly routing and rundown lifetime are unchanged ([paging route](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:1891>), [AdvanceOnly route](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:2026>), [seal worker](</home/victor/Work/safeupload-wt-sol/driver/SafeUpload.Minifilter/StageStream.c:2268>)).

**Conditions before qualification:**

- Four exact-source WDK builds—normal and feature, Debug and Release—with PREfast and ApiValidator clean.
- A fresh runtime-Verifier run on the specified Windows 10 19045 fixed-NTFS setup, using the unchanged gate, actor, case, and stimulus. It must show the extending empty-file `CreateFileMapping` and `MapView` complete, source close succeeds while the view remains usable, and subsequent view disposal, cleanup, and sealing complete. Capture the Verifier state and results.

I made no edits and ran no builds or VM tests. This is conditional source-level acceptance, not runtime qualification.
